{"success": true, "name": "claude-code", "description": "Delegate coding to Claude Code CLI (features, PRs).", "tags": ["Coding-Agent", "Claude", "Anthropic", "Code-Review", "Refactoring", "PTY", "Automation"], "related_skills": ["codex", "hermes-agent", "opencode"], "content": "---\nname: claude-code\ndescription: \"Delegate coding to Claude Code CLI (features, PRs).\"\nversion: 2.2.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent + Teknium\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [Coding-Agent, Claude, Anthropic, Code-Review, Refactoring, PTY, Automation]\n    related_skills: [codex, hermes-agent, opencode]\n---\n\n# Claude Code — Hermes Orchestration Guide\n\nDelegate coding tasks to [Claude Code](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference) (Anthropic's autonomous coding agent CLI) via the Hermes terminal. Claude Code v2.x can read files, write code, run shell commands, spawn subagents, and manage git workflows autonomously.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- **Install:** `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code`\n- **Auth:** run `claude` once to log in (browser OAuth for Pro/Max, or set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`)\n- **Console auth:** `claude auth login --console` for API key billing\n- **SSO auth:** `claude auth login --sso` for Enterprise\n- **Check status:** `claude auth status` (JSON) or `claude auth status --text` (human-readable)\n- **Health check:** `claude doctor` — checks auto-updater and installation health\n- **Version check:** `claude --version` (requires v2.x+)\n- **Update:** `claude update` or `claude upgrade`\n\n## Two Orchestration Modes\n\nHermes interacts with Claude Code in two fundamentally different ways. Choose based on the task.\n\n### Mode 1: Print Mode (`-p`) — Non-Interactive (PREFERRED for most tasks)\n\nPrint mode runs a one-shot task, returns the result, and exits. No PTY needed. No interactive prompts. This is the cleanest integration path.\n\n``​`\nterminal(command=\"claude -p 'Add error handling to all API calls in src/' --allowedTools 'Read,Edit' --max-turns 10\", workdir=\"/path/to/project\", timeout=120)\n``​`\n\n**When to use print mode:**\n- One-shot coding tasks (fix a bug, add a feature, refactor)\n- CI/CD automation and scripting\n- Structured data extraction with `--json-schema`\n- Piped input processing (`cat file | claude -p \"analyze this\"`)\n- Any task where you don't need multi-turn conversation\n\n**Print mode skips ALL interactive dialogs** — no workspace trust prompt, no permission confirmations. This makes it ideal for automation.\n\n### Mode 2: Interactive PTY via tmux — Multi-Turn Sessions\n\nInteractive mode gives you a full conversational REPL where you can send follow-up prompts, use slash commands, and watch Claude work in real time. **Requires tmux orchestration.**\n\n``​`\n# Start a tmux session\nterminal(command=\"tmux new-session -d -s claude-work -x 140 -y 40\")\n\n# Launch Claude Code inside it\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'cd /path/to/project && claude' Enter\")\n\n# Wait for startup, then send your task\n# (after ~3-5 seconds for the welcome screen)\nterminal(command=\"sleep 5 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'Refactor the auth module to use JWT tokens' Enter\")\n\n# Monitor progress by capturing the pane\nterminal(command=\"sleep 15 && tmux capture-pane -t claude-work -p -S -50\")\n\n# Send follow-up tasks\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'Now add unit tests for the new JWT code' Enter\")\n\n# Exit when done\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work '/exit' Enter\")\n``​`\n\n**When to use interactive mode:**\n- Multi-turn iterative work (refactor → review → fix → test cycle)\n- Tasks requiring human-in-the-loop decisions\n- Exploratory coding sessions\n- When you need to use Claude's slash commands (`/compact`, `/review`, `/model`)\n\n## PTY Dialog Handling (CRITICAL for Interactive Mode)\n\nClaude Code presents up to two confirmation dialogs on first launch. You MUST handle these via tmux send-keys:\n\n### Dialog 1: Workspace Trust (first visit to a directory)\n``​`\n❯ 1. Yes, I trust this folder    ← DEFAULT (just press Enter)\n  2. No, exit\n``​`\n**Handling:** `tmux send-keys -t <session> Enter` — default selection is correct.\n\n### Dialog 2: Bypass Permissions Warning (only with --dangerously-skip-permissions)\n``​`\n❯ 1. No, exit                    ← DEFAULT (WRONG choice!)\n  2. Yes, I accept\n``​`\n**Handling:** Must navigate DOWN first, then Enter:\n``​`\ntmux send-keys -t <session> Down && sleep 0.3 && tmux send-keys -t <session> Enter\n``​`\n\n### Robust Dialog Handling Pattern\n``​`\n# Launch with permissions bypass\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t claude-work 'claude --dangerously-skip-permissions \\\"your task\\\"' Enter\")\n\n# Handle trust dialog (Enter for default \"Yes\")\nterminal(command=\"sleep 4 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work Enter\")\n\n# Handle permissions dialog (Down then Enter for \"Yes, I accept\")\nterminal(command=\"sleep 3 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work Down && sleep 0.3 && tmux send-keys -t claude-work Enter\")\n\n# Now wait for Claude to work\nterminal(command=\"sleep 15 && tmux capture-pane -t claude-work -p -S -60\")\n``​`\n\n**Note:** After the first trust acceptance for a directory, the trust dialog won't appear again. Only the permissions dialog recurs each time you use `--dangerously-skip-permissions`.\n\n## CLI Subcommands\n\n| Subcommand | Purpose |\n|------------|---------|\n| `claude` | Start interactive REPL |\n| `claude \"query\"` | Start REPL with initial prompt |\n| `claude -p \"query\"` | Print mode (non-interactive, exits when done) |\n| `cat file \\| claude -p \"query\"` | Pipe content as stdin context |\n| `claude -c` | Continue the most recent conversation in this directory |\n| `claude -r \"id\"` | Resume a specific session by ID or name |\n| `claude auth login` | Sign in (add `--console` for API billing, `--sso` for Enterprise) |\n| `claude auth status` | Check login status (returns JSON; `--text` for human-readable) |

Hermes works with any LLM provider (OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI, DeepSeek, local models, and 15+ others) and runs on Linux, macOS, and WSL.\n\nWhat makes Hermes different:\n\n- **Self-improving through skills** — Hermes learns from experience by saving reusable procedures as skills. When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. Credential pools rotate across multiple API keys automatically.\n- **Profiles** — run multiple independent Hermes instances with isolated configs, sessions, skills, and memory.\n- **Extensible** — plugins, MCP servers, custom tools, webhook triggers, cron scheduling, and the full Python ecosystem.\n\nPeople use Hermes for software development, research, system administration, data analysis, content creation, home automation, and anything else that benefits from an AI agent with persistent context and full system access.\n\n**This skill helps you work with Hermes Agent effectively** — setting it up, configuring features, spawning additional agent instances, troubleshooting issues, finding the right commands and settings, and understanding how the system works when you need to extend or contribute to it.\n\n**Docs:** https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/\n\n## Quick Start\n\n``​`bash\n# Install\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard\nhermes setup\n\n# Change model/provider\nhermes model\n\n# Check health\nhermes doctor\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## CLI Reference\n\n### Global Flags\n\n``​`\nhermes [flags] [command]\n\n  --version, -V             Show version\n  --resume, -r SESSION      Resume session by ID or title\n  --continue, -c [NAME]     Resume by name, or most recent session\n  --worktree, -w            Isolated git worktree mode (parallel agents)\n  --skills, -s SKILL        Preload skills (comma-separate or repeat)\n  --profile, -p NAME        Use a named profile\n  --yolo                    Skip dangerous command approval\n  --pass-session-id         Include session ID in system prompt\n``​`\n\nNo subcommand defaults to `chat`.\n\n### Chat\n\n``​`\nhermes chat [flags]\n  -q, --query TEXT          Single query, non-interactive\n  -m, --model MODEL         Model (e.g. anthropic/claude-sonnet-4)\n  -t, --toolsets LIST       Comma-separated toolsets\n  --provider PROVIDER       Force provider (openrouter, anthropic, nous, etc.)\n  -v, --verbose             Verbose output\n  -Q, --quiet               Suppress banner, spinner, tool previews\n  --checkpoints             Enable filesystem checkpoints (/rollback)\n  --source TAG              Session source tag (default: cli)\n``​`\n\n### Configuration\n\n``​`\nhermes setup [section]      Interactive wizard (model|terminal|gateway|tools|agent)\nhermes model                Interactive model/provider picker\nhermes config               View current config\nhermes config edit          Open config.yaml in $EDITOR\nhermes config set KEY VAL   Set a config value\nhermes config path          Print config.yaml path\nhermes config env-path      Print .env path\nhermes config check         Check for missing/outdated config\nhermes config migrate       Update config with new options\nhermes login [--provider P] OAuth login (nous, openai-codex)\nhermes logout               Clear stored auth\nhermes doctor [--fix]       Check dependencies and config\nhermes status [--all]       Show component status\n``​`\n\n### Tools & Skills\n\n``​`\nhermes tools                Interactive tool enable/disable (curses UI)\nhermes tools list           Show all tools and status\nhermes tools enable NAME    Enable a toolset\nhermes tools disable NAME   Disable a toolset\n\nhermes skills list          List installed skills\nhermes skills search QUERY  Search the skills hub\nhermes skills install ID    Install a skill (ID can be a hub identifier OR a direct https://…/SKILL.md URL; pass --name to override when frontmatter has no name)\nhermes skills inspect ID    Preview without installing\nhermes skills config        Enable/disable skills per platform\nhermes skills check         Check for updates\nhermes skills update        Update outdated skills\nhermes skills unins

Keep durable facts/preferences in Hermes memory; keep operational rolling state in files.\n\n## Current files\n\n- Daily ToM sync: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`\n- Daily ToM context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py`\n- Email context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/email-tom-context.py`\n- Slack collector: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_collect.py`\n- Slack deterministic pre-filter: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py`\n- Briefing context builder: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`\n- Rolling state: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/open_topics.md`\n- Briefing policy: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/BRIEFING_POLICY.md`\n- Final brief archive directory: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/`\n- Google security policy: `/opt/data/google-accounts/SECURITY_POLICY.md`\n- Attack-vector checklist: `/opt/data/security/ATTACK_VECTOR_CHECKLIST.md`\n\n## Model-routing posture\n\n- Dumb/API work: script-only, no LLM.\n- Slack crawl collection: no-agent Python script.\n- ToM sync: no-agent Python script.\n- Email/ToM/Slack context extraction: deterministic scripts.\n- Final executive synthesis: heavier model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6`) because it requires judgment.\n- Simple health summaries: lightweight model (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5`).\n\n## Briefing structure\n\nSix sections, always in this order:\n\n1. **Executive summary** — 3-5 bullets max. Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. No action today. One paragraph or less per item.\n4. **Deliberately excluded** — one-liner list showing what the filter caught and why. Builds trust.\n5. **Suggested ToM additions — approval required** — formatted ToM-style entries. Ask DJ directly for each.\n6. **Watchlist / carry-forward** — compact rolling state for next briefing.\n\nSee `references/slack-filter-calibration.md` for detailed content filtering rules.\n\n## Calibration rules (v3 — validated [REDACTED_PHONE])\n\n### The DJ relevance test\nEvery item must answer: **\"Why does DJ need to know this, and what decision or action follows?\"**\nIf the answer is weak → downgrade to \"Worth knowing\" or exclude. Do not fill the briefing with items that merely exist.\n\n### Hard exclusions (never include)\n- **Slackbot app install requests** — DJ is a workspace super admin and receives these automatically. They are IT's job. Hard-exclude at filter level, never surface in briefing.\n- **Pure bot test results / CI channels** — waves-dev-test-automation, test-android-results, backend-dev-testify-automation, application-status, plattest-012n3.\n- **Cleaning/housekeeping ops channels** — below DJ level.\n- **Platform degradation bot floods** — repeated bot-only degraded/outage lines with no escalation owner or recovery status.\n\n### Always include\n- **DMs and MPIMs** — always surface. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting. Never quote one line and guess meaning.\n- **Exact Daily ToM entities** — Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, Olaya, Hubspot, F&B, Board deck, etc.\n- **Deal/acquisition/project channels** — proj-society-wynwood-acquisition, proj-mena, proj-easton, and similar.\n- **Money, legal, approval, deadline, investor, board signals** — wire, drawdown, capital, contract, signature, DD, due diligence, data room.\n\n### Include with judgment\n- **Tour/prospect signals in leasing channels** — people waiting for tours, prospect visits, staffing gaps at open houses. These ARE valuable to DJ even though \"leasing\" is otherwise a weak keyword.\n- **Engineering items with a named ToM entity** — product naming decisions (e.g., \"Society Wynwood → Flow Wynwood\"), blockers, or product decisions linked to active deals.\n- **Notable engineering discussions** — include when there is a real decision or entity; exclude low-context chatter (PR merges, minor code comments, identity creation, color fixes).\n\n### DM / group-DM handling\n- Always surface DMs/MPIMs.\n- Read **all context lines** provided before drawing any conclusion.\n- If a message is ambiguous without context (e.g., \"let's wind him down the week after next\"), report: what you know (participants, instruction given), what you don't know (subject), and provide the link. **Do not guess.**\n- If context is insufficient: \"Group DM with [names] mentions [instruction] — subject not identifiable from available context. Link: [url].\"\n\n### False inference rule\n- **Never connect unrelated items** as if they are related.\n- Example: A Vercel access approval for a MENA app does NOT mean MENA KPI/Hubspot/revenue diagnostic work is unblocked — they are completely separate. Only state connections that are **explicitly** supported by the source mat

### assistant

```text
That alert came from `chief_operational_health.py`, not from the model.

What happened:

- The health check runs `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py`.
- It expects the output to contain this exact markdown marker:

``​`md
## Daily Top of Mind Context
``​`

- The Daily ToM script failed to fetch the Google Doc because the runtime Python used by `/opt/data/scripts/google-account` was missing Google API packages:

``​`text
ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'googleapiclient'
``​`

- The ToM context script caught that failure and printed its fallback header:

``​`md
## Daily ToM Context
Unable to fetch Daily ToM: CalledProcessError
``​`

- That fallback header was slightly different from what the health check expected, so Chief reported “missing expected markdown marker.”

I fixed both issues:

- Created a dedicated Google API Python venv under `/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv`.
- Installed the needed Google API packages there.
- Updated `/opt/data/scripts/google-account` to use that `/opt/data` venv.
- Fixed the fallback headers in `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py` so they use the same expected marker even when Google fetch fails.

Verified now:

``​`text
Daily ToM context: OK
Email ToM context: OK
Filtered Slack context: OK
Full smart brief context: OK
Status: OK
``​`

So the warning was real but non-blocking: the health check caught a broken dependency plus a too-strict marker mismatch. It’s fixed now.
```

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something wrong, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <either \"none unless this repeats\" or specific action required>\n``​`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- \"DJ action\" = either \"none, Hermes should fix this\" or an explicit ask. Never omit this line.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Operational health check pattern\n\nFor monitoring several file freshness checks + script probes in Python:\n\n``​`python\nISSUES: list[str] = []\nLINES: list[str] = []\n\ndef check_file(path, max_age_h=None, max_size_kb=None, min_size=1):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if not p.exists():\n        ISSUES.append(f\"Missing file: {path}\")\n        LINES.append(f\"{path}: MISSING\")\n        return\n    age = (time.time() - p.stat().st_mtime) / 3600\n    LINES.append(f\"{path}: {p.stat().st_size} bytes, age {age:.1f}h\")\n    if p.stat().st_size < min_size:\n        ISSUES.append(f\"File appears empty: {path}\")\n    if max_age_h and age > max_age_h:\n        I

Just email, or also\nCalendar/Drive/Sheets/Docs?\"**\n\n- **Email only** → They don't need this skill at all. Use the `himalaya` skill\n  instead — it works with a Gmail App Password (Settings → Security → App\n  Passwords) and takes 2 minutes to set up. No Google Cloud project needed.\n  Load the himalaya skill and follow its setup instructions.\n\n- **Email + Calendar** → Continue with this skill, but use\n  `--services email,calendar` during auth so the consent screen only asks for\n  the scopes they actually need.\n\n- **Calendar/Drive/Sheets/Docs only** → Continue with this skill and use a\n  narrower `--services` set like `calendar,drive,sheets,docs`.\n\n- **Full Workspace access** → Continue with this skill and use the default\n  `all` service set.\n\n**Question 2: \"Does your Google account use Advanced Protection (hardware\nsecurity keys required to sign in)? If you're not sure, you probably don't\n— it's something you would have explicitly enrolled in.\"**\n\n- **No / Not sure** → Normal setup. Continue below.\n- **Yes** → Their Workspace admin must add the OAuth client ID to the org's\n  allowed apps list before Step 4 will work. Let them know upfront.\n\n### Step 2: Create OAuth credentials (one-time, ~5 minutes)\n\nTell the user:\n\n> You need a Google Cloud OAuth client. This is a one-time setup:\n>\n> 1. Create or select a project:\n>    https://console.cloud.google.com/projectselector2/home/dashboard\n> 2. Application type: \"Desktop app\" → Create\n> 5. Download the JSON file and tell me the file path\n>\n> Important Hermes CLI note: if the file path starts with `/`, do NOT send only the bare path as its own message in the CLI, because it can be mistaken for a slash command. Examples:\n\n``​`bash\n$GSETUP --auth-url --services email,calendar --format json\n$GSETUP --auth-url --services calendar,drive,sheets,docs --format json\n$GSETUP --auth-url --services all --format json\n``​`\n\nThis returns JSON with an `auth_url` field and also saves the exact URL to\n`~/.hermes/google_oauth_last_url.txt`.\n\nAgent rules for this step:\n- Extract the `auth_url` field and send that exact URL to the user as a single line.\n- Tell the user that the browser will likely fail on `http://localhost:1` after approval, and that this is expected.\n- Tell them to copy the ENTIRE redirected URL from the browser address bar.\n- If the user gets `Error 403: access_denied`, send them directly to `https://console.cloud.google.com/auth/audience` to add themselves as a test user.\n\n### Step 4: Exchange the code\n\nThe user will paste back either a URL like `http://localhost:1/?code=4/0A...&scope=...`\nor just the code string. otherwise insert after last item.\nfor i in range(section_idx+1, next_section_idx):\n    if paras[i].text == '':\n        insert_at = paras[i].start\n        break\ntext = f'BOD demos [n:{task_id}]\\n'\ndocs.documents().batchUpdate(documentId=mod.DOC_ID, body={'requests':[{'insertText': {'location': {'index': insert_at}, 'text': text}}]}).execute()\nprint(f'Added to {label}: {text.strip()}')\n","path":"/tmp/add_bod_demos.py"}

The briefing reads DJ's Daily ToM first and uses it as the priority lens for Slack + email review.\n\n## Safety rules\n\n1. Never send, reply, forward, invite, share, comment, or notify anyone.\n2. Never write Slack/email-derived items into the Daily ToM doc without DJ's explicit approval.\n3. Suggested ToM additions are approval-required suggestions only.\n4. Do not persist raw Slack/email contents to Hermes memory.\n5. Keep durable facts/preferences in Hermes memory; keep operational rolling state in files.\n\n## Current files\n\n- Daily ToM sync: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`\n- Daily ToM context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py`\n- Email context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/email-tom-context.py`\n- Slack collector: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_collect.py`\n- Slack deterministic pre-filter: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py`\n- Briefing context builder: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`\n- Rolling state: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/open_topics.md`\n- Briefing policy: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/BRIEFING_POLICY.md`\n- Final brief archive directory: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/`\n- Google security policy: `/opt/data/google-accounts/SECURITY_POLICY.md`\n- Attack-vector checklist: `/opt/data/security/ATTACK_VECTOR_CHECKLIST.md`\n\n## Model-routing posture\n\n- Dumb/API work: script-only, no LLM.\n- Slack crawl collection: no-agent Python script.\n- ToM sync: no-agent Python script.\n- Email/ToM/Slack context extraction: deterministic scripts.\n- Final executive synthesis: heavier model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6`) because it requires judgment.\n- Simple health summaries: lightweight model (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5`).\n\n## Briefing structure\n\nSix sections, always in this order:\n\n1. **Executive summary** — 3-5 bullets max. Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. No action today. One paragraph or less per item.\n4. **Deliberately excluded** — one-liner list showing what the filter caught and why. Builds trust.\n5. **Suggested ToM additions — approval required** — formatted ToM-style entries. Ask DJ directly for each.\n6. **Watchlist / carry-forward** — compact rolling state for next briefing.\n\nSee `references/slack-filter-calibration.md` for detailed content filtering rules.\n\n## Calibration rules (v3 — validated [REDACTED_PHONE])\n\n### The DJ relevance test\nEvery item must answer: **\"Why does DJ need to know this, and what decision or action follows?\"**\nIf the answer is weak → downgrade to \"Worth knowing\" or exclude. Do not fill the briefing with items that merely exist.\n\n### Hard exclusions (never include)\n- **Slackbot app install requests** — DJ is a workspace super admin and receives these automatically. They are IT's job. Hard-exclude at filter level, never surface in briefing.\n- **Pure bot test results / CI channels** — waves-dev-test-automation, test-android-results, backend-dev-testify-automation, application-status, plattest-012n3.\n- **Cleaning/housekeeping ops channels** — below DJ level.\n- **Platform degradation bot floods** — repeated bot-only degraded/outage lines with no escalation owner or recovery status.\n\n### Always include\n- **DMs and MPIMs** — always surface. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting. Never quote one line and guess meaning.\n- **Exact Daily ToM entities** — Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, Olaya, Hubspot, F&B, Board deck, etc.\n- **Deal/acquisition/project channels** — proj-society-wynwood-acquisition, proj-mena, proj-easton, and similar.\n- **Money, legal, approval, deadline, investor, board signals** — wire, drawdown, capital, contract, signature, DD, due diligence, data room.\n\n### Include with judgment\n- **Tour/prospect signals in leasing channels** — people waiting for tours, prospect visits, staffing gaps at open houses. These ARE valuable to DJ even though \"leasing\" is otherwise a weak keyword.\n- **Engineering items with a named ToM entity** — product naming decisions (e.g., \"Society Wynwood → Flow Wynwood\"), blockers, or product decisions linked to active deals.\n- **Notable engineering discussions** — include when there is a real decision or entity; exclude low-context chatter (PR merges, minor code comments, identity creation, color fixes).\n\n### DM / group-DM handling\n- Always surface DMs/MPIMs.\n- Read **all context lines** provided before drawing any conclusion.\n- If a message is ambiguous without context (e.g., \"let's wind him down the week after next\"), report: what you know (participants, instruction given), what you don't know (subject), and provide the link. **Do not guess.**\n- If context is insufficient: \"Group DM with [names] mentions [instruction] — subject not identifiable from available context. Link: [url].\"\n\n### False inference rule\n- **Never connect unrelated items** as if they are related.\n- Example: A Vercel access approval for a MENA app does NOT mean MENA KPI/Hubspot/revenue diagnostic work is unblocked — they are completely separate. Only state connections that are **explicitly** supported by the source mat

{"path":"/tmp/inspect_tom_styles.py","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\nimport importlib.util, sys, json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nSCRIPT = '/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py'\nspec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('daily_tom_sync', SCRIPT)\nmod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\nsys.modules['daily_tom_sync'] = mod\nspec.loader.exec_module(mod)\n\ndocs = mod.build('docs','v1', credentials=mod.get_creds(mod.DEFAULT_HERMES_HOME), cache_discovery=False)\ndoc = docs.documents().get(documentId=mod.DOC_ID).execute()\nparas = []\nfor el in doc.get('body', {}).get('content', []):\n    para = el.get('paragraph')\n    if not para:\n        continue\n    text = ''.join((pe.get('textRun') or {}).get('content','') for pe in para.get('elements', [])).rstrip('\\n')\n    if not text and len(paras) > 80:\n        continue\n    style = para.get('paragraphStyle', {})\n    elem_styles = []\n    for pe in para.get('elements', []):\n        tr = pe.get('textRun')\n        if tr:\n            elem_styles.append({'range':[pe.get('startIndex'), pe.get('endIndex')], 'content': tr.get('content','').rstrip('\\n'), 'textStyle': tr.get('textStyle', {})})\n    paras.append({'text': text, 'start': el.get('startIndex'), 'end': el.get('endIndex'), 'pstyle': style, 'runs': elem_styles})\n\n# Print top 70 paragraphs and styles compactly\nfor i,p in enumerate(paras[:70]):\n    print(f\"--- {i} {p['start']}..{p['end']} {p['text']!r}\")\n    keep = {k:v for k,v in p['pstyle'].items() if k in ['namedStyleType','alignment','indentStart','indentFirstLine','spaceAbove','spaceBelow','lineSpacing']}\n    print('PSTYLE', json.dumps(keep, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True))\n    # first text run style only\n    if p['runs']:\n        ts = p['runs'][0]['textStyle']\n        keep_ts = {k:v for k,v in ts.items() if k in ['bold','italic','fontSize','weightedFontFamily','foregroundColor','backgroundColor','link']}\n        print('TSTYLE', json.dumps(keep_ts, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True))\n"}

The desired format is: date heading = Heading 2,\n    all section labels/tasks/blanks = Normal text.\n    \"\"\"\n    requests: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    idx = start_index\n    for line_no, line in enumerate(text.splitlines(keepends=True)):\n        end = idx + len(line)\n        if end > idx:\n            requests.append({\n                \"updateParagraphStyle\": {\n                    \"range\": {\"startIndex\": idx, \"endIndex\": end},\n                    \"paragraphStyle\": {\"namedStyleType\": \"HEADING_2\" if line_no == 0 else \"NORMAL_TEXT\"},\n                    \"fields\": \"namedStyleType\",\n                }\n            })\n        idx = end\n    return requests\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n"}

{"content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\nimport datetime as dt\nimport importlib.util, sys, json\nfrom zoneinfo import ZoneInfo\n\nSCRIPT = '/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py'\nspec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('daily_tom_sync', SCRIPT)\nmod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\nsys.modules['daily_tom_sync'] = mod\nspec.loader.exec_module(mod)\n\ntoday = dt.datetime.now(ZoneInfo('America/New_York')).date()\nlabel = today.strftime('%B ') + str(today.day) + today.strftime(', %Y')\n\ndocs = mod.build('docs','v1', credentials=mod.get_creds(mod.DEFAULT_HERMES_HOME), cache_discovery=False)\ndoc = docs.documents().get(documentId=mod.DOC_ID).execute()\nparas = mod.extract_paragraphs(doc)\nstart = next((i for i,p in enumerate(paras) if p.text == label), None)\nif start is None:\n    raise SystemExit(f'Today section not found: {label}')\nend = next((i for i in range(start+1, len(paras)) if mod.DATE_RE.match(paras[i].text)), len(paras))\nrequests = []\n# Date heading remains Heading 2.\nrequests.append({\n    'updateParagraphStyle': {\n        'range': {'startIndex': paras[start].start, 'endIndex': paras[start].end},\n        'paragraphStyle': {'namedStyleType': 'HEADING_2'},\n        'fields': 'namedStyleType',\n    }\n})\n# Everything else in today's generated day should be Normal text.\nfor p in paras[start+1:end]:\n    if p.end > p.start:\n        requests.append({\n            'updateParagraphStyle': {\n                'range': {'startIndex': p.start, 'endIndex': p.end},\n                'paragraphStyle': {'namedStyleType': 'NORMAL_TEXT'},\n                'fields': 'namedStyleType',\n            }\n        })\nif requests:\n    docs.documents().batchUpdate(documentId=mod.DOC_ID, body={'requests': requests}).execute()\nprint(json.dumps({'status':'formatted', 'section': label, 'paragraphs_updated': len(requests)}, indent=2))\n","path":"/tmp/fix_today_tom_styles.py"}
```

```text
Yes — I found the issue and fixed it.

What was happening:
- The sync script inserted the new day right after `[Next date]`.
- Google Docs `insertText` inherits paragraph style from the insertion point.
- That caused the whole generated day — section labels and task lines — to become **Heading 2**, instead of only the date being Heading 2.

I changed `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py` so every future generated day explicitly formats:
- Date line: **Heading 2**
- Blank lines, section labels, and task rows: **Normal text**

I also repaired the already-created current section:
- `May 29, 2026`: Heading 2
- `[Professional]`, tasks including `BOD demos`, MENA/Others/Personal: Normal text

Verified:
- Current Google Doc paragraph styles now match the prior formatting pattern.
- `daily-tom-sync.py` compiles.
- Chief health check is clean: `Status: OK`.

The briefing reads DJ's Daily ToM first and uses it as the priority lens for Slack + email review.\n\n## Safety rules\n\n1. Never send, reply, forward, invite, share, comment, or notify anyone.\n2. Never write Slack/email-derived items into the Daily ToM doc without DJ's explicit approval.\n3. Suggested ToM additions are approval-required suggestions only.\n4. Do not persist raw Slack/email contents to Hermes memory.\n5. Keep durable facts/preferences in Hermes memory; keep operational rolling state in files.\n\n## Current files\n\n- Daily ToM sync: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`\n- Daily ToM context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py`\n- Email context extractor: `/opt/data/scripts/email-tom-context.py`\n- Slack collector: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_collect.py`\n- Slack deterministic pre-filter: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py`\n- Briefing context builder: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`\n- Rolling state: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/open_topics.md`\n- Briefing policy: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/BRIEFING_POLICY.md`\n- Final brief archive directory: `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/`\n- Google security policy: `/opt/data/google-accounts/SECURITY_POLICY.md`\n- Attack-vector checklist: `/opt/data/security/ATTACK_VECTOR_CHECKLIST.md`\n\n## Model-routing posture\n\n- Dumb/API work: script-only, no LLM.\n- Slack crawl collection: no-agent Python script.\n- ToM sync: no-agent Python script.\n- Email/ToM/Slack context extraction: deterministic scripts.\n- Final executive synthesis: heavier model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6`) because it requires judgment.\n- Simple health summaries: lightweight model (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5`).\n\n## Briefing structure\n\nSix sections, always in this order:\n\n1. **Executive summary** — 3-5 bullets max. Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. No action today. One paragraph or less per item.\n4. **Deliberately excluded** — one-liner list showing what the filter caught and why. Builds trust.\n5. **Suggested ToM additions — approval required** — formatted ToM-style entries. Ask DJ directly for each.\n6. **Watchlist / carry-forward** — compact rolling state for next briefing.\n\nSee `references/slack-filter-calibration.md` for detailed content filtering rules.\n\n## Calibration rules (v3 — validated [REDACTED_PHONE])\n\n### The DJ relevance test\nEvery item must answer: **\"Why does DJ need to know this, and what decision or action follows?\"**\nIf the answer is weak → downgrade to \"Worth knowing\" or exclude. Do not fill the briefing with items that merely exist.\n\n### Hard exclusions (never include)\n- **Slackbot app install requests** — DJ is a workspace super admin and receives these automatically. They are IT's job. Hard-exclude at filter level, never surface in briefing.\n- **Pure bot test results / CI channels** — waves-dev-test-automation, test-android-results, backend-dev-testify-automation, application-status, plattest-012n3.\n- **Cleaning/housekeeping ops channels** — below DJ level.\n- **Platform degradation bot floods** — repeated bot-only degraded/outage lines with no escalation owner or recovery status.\n\n### Always include\n- **DMs and MPIMs** — always surface. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting. Never quote one line and guess meaning.\n- **Exact Daily ToM entities** — Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, Olaya, Hubspot, F&B, Board deck, etc.\n- **Deal/acquisition/project channels** — proj-society-wynwood-acquisition, proj-mena, proj-easton, and similar.\n- **Money, legal, approval, deadline, investor, board signals** — wire, drawdown, capital, contract, signature, DD, due diligence, data room.\n\n### Include with judgment\n- **Tour/prospect signals in leasing channels** — people waiting for tours, prospect visits, staffing gaps at open houses. These ARE valuable to DJ even though \"leasing\" is otherwise a weak keyword.\n- **Engineering items with a named ToM entity** — product naming decisions (e.g., \"Society Wynwood → Flow Wynwood\"), blockers, or product decisions linked to active deals.\n- **Notable engineering discussions** — include when there is a real decision or entity; exclude low-context chatter (PR merges, minor code comments, identity creation, color fixes).\n\n### DM / group-DM handling\n- Always surface DMs/MPIMs.\n- Read **all context lines** provided before drawing any conclusion.\n- If a message is ambiguous without context (e.g., \"let's wind him down the week after next\"), report: what you know (participants, instruction given), what you don't know (subject), and provide the link. **Do not guess.**\n- If context is insufficient: \"Group DM with [names] mentions [instruction] — subject not identifiable from available context. Link: [url].\"\n\n### False inference rule\n- **Never connect unrelated items** as if they are related.\n- Example: A Vercel access approval for a MENA app does NOT mean MENA KPI/Hubspot/revenue diagnostic work is unblocked — they are completely separate. Only state connections that are **explicitly** supported by the source mat

Otherwise a genuine upstream fetch failure can be misreported as a markdown-format issue.\n\n## Manual run for today's ET date\n\nWhen DJ asks to \"run it for today\" and side effects are explicitly intended:\n\n``​`bash\nTODAY=\"$(TZ=America/New_York date +%F)\"\n/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python /opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py --date \"$TODAY\" --apply\n/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py | sed -n '1,80p'\n/opt/data/scripts/chief_operational_health.py\n``​`\n\nReport the JSON `status`, `date`, `latest_source_section`, counts, groups, then confirm the context extractor sees the new current section and health is OK.\n\n## Runtime/dependency path check\n\nThe Google Workspace scripts may depend on Python Google API packages. If system `python3` lacks those packages, prefer a persistent `/opt/data` runtime instead of installing into transient/system Python:\n\n``​`bash\nuv venv /opt/data/google-accounts/.venv\nuv pip install --python /opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python \\\n  google-api-python-client google-auth-oauthlib google-auth-httplib2\n``​`\n\nThen ensure wrappers that invoke Google APIs use `/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python` with a `python3` fallback. This keeps the integration tied to the persistent Railway volume and avoids code changes under `/opt/hermes`.\n\n## Schedule shape\n\nFor the Daily ToM 5AM ET job, avoid hourly cron wakeups. Use UTC candidate hours for DST plus the wrapper's ET/date guard:\n\n``​`cron\n0 9,10 * * *\n``​`\n\nThe EDT candidate is 09:00 UTC; the EST candidate is 10:00 UTC. Only the matching 5AM ET candidate should do work; the other should exit silently.\n\n## Google Docs style preservation\n\nWhen `daily-tom-sync.py` creates a new day, plain `insertText` inherits the paragraph style at the insertion point. Because the insertion point is near `[Next date]`, this can accidentally make every inserted paragraph `HEADING_2`. Preserve the established document pattern explicitly:\n\n- Date line: `HEADING_2`\n- Blank lines, section labels (`[Professional]`, etc.), and task rows: `NORMAL_TEXT`\n\nPreferred implementation pattern in `daily-tom-sync.py`:\n\n1. Build `inserted_text = new_section + \"\\n\"`.\n2. Add the `insertText` request first.\n3. Add one `updateParagraphStyle` request per inserted paragraph over the inserted ranges.\n4. Set only `fields: \"namedStyleType\"` so other doc formatting is not disturbed.\n\nIf a generated day already has bad styles, repair just that day's paragraph ranges: keep the date paragraph `HEADING_2`; set all paragraphs until the next date section to `NORMAL_TEXT`. Verify by reading Docs API `paragraph.paragraphStyle.namedStyleType`, not by eyeballing extracted plain text.\n\n## Manual task additions\n\nWhen DJ explicitly asks to add a ToM item to today's list, it is an approved Docs edit. Add it to the current top dated section, usually under `[Professional]` unless the request clearly names another section. Preserve the ToM task-id convention by appending a fresh `[n:<id>]`, and verify with `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py` that the item appears under the expected section.\n\nBe careful with Google Docs insertion indexes: fetch the live doc, find today's date paragraph, find the section paragraph and next section/date boundary, then insert before that section's trailing blank line or before the next section. Do not use stale indexes from a previous read after making edits.\n\n## Verification checklist\n\n- `bash -n /opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-daily-5am-et.sh`\n- `/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python -m py_compile /opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`\n- `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-context.py` starts with `## Daily Top of Mind Context`\n- Context says `Current ToM section: <today's date>` after an applied run\n- If styles were changed: inspect Docs API paragraph styles for the current day (`HEADING_2` date, `NORMAL_TEXT` body)\n- `/opt/data/scripts/chief_operational_health.py` ends with `Status: OK`\n- `hermes cron list` shows Daily ToM enabled with schedule `0 9,10 * * *`\n", "file_type": ".md"}

{"path":"/tmp/normalize_tom_markers.py","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Normalize DJ's Daily ToM keyboard shorthand in the Google Doc.\n\nConverts leading x/[x] to ✅ and leading >/ [>] to ↗️ across historical ToM\nitems. Skips title/date/section marker paragraphs and avoids uppercase X task\nnames like \"XM comp\".\n\"\"\"\nimport importlib.util\nimport json\nimport re\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nSCRIPT = '/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py'\nspec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('daily_tom_sync', SCRIPT)\nmod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\nsys.modules['daily_tom_sync'] = mod\nspec.loader.exec_module(mod)\n\nDONE_PREFIX = re.compile(r'^(?:\\[x\\]\\s*|[xX]\\s+|x(?=[A-Z0-9]))')\nPROG_PREFIX = re.compile(r'^(?:\\[>\\]\\s*|>\\s*)')\n\ndef replacement_for(text: str):\n    stripped = text.lstrip()\n    leading_ws = len(text) - len(stripped)\n    if not stripped:\n        return None\n    # Skip non-task structural lines.\n    if stripped == '[Next date]' or stripped == '[Parking Lot]' or mod.SECTION_RE.match(stripped) or mod.DATE_RE.match(stripped):\n        return None\n    if stripped.startswith('✅') or stripped.startswith('↗️'):\n        return None\n    m = DONE_PREFIX.match(stripped)\n    if m:\n        rest = stripped[m.end():].lstrip()\n        if rest:\n            return leading_ws, m.end(), '✅ ' + rest\n    m = PROG_PREFIX.match(stripped)\n    if m:\n        rest = stripped[m.end():].lstrip()\n        if rest:\n            return leading_ws, m.end(), '↗️ ' + rest\n    return None\n\ndocs = mod.build('docs', 'v1', credentials=mod.get_creds(mod.DEFAULT_HERMES_HOME), cache_discovery=False)\ndoc = docs.documents().get(documentId=mod.DOC_ID).execute()\nparas = mod.extract_paragraphs(doc)\nchanges = []\nrequests = []\n\nfor p in paras:\n    repl = replacement_for(p.text)\n    if not repl:\n        continue\n    leading_ws, prefix_len, new_text = repl\n    start = p.start + leading_ws\n    end = p.end - 1  # exclude paragraph newline\n    if end <= start:\n        continue\n    changes.append({'old': p.text, 'new': (' ' * leading_ws) + new_text, 'start': p.start, 'end': p.end})\n    # Reverse order batch means indexes remain valid.\n    requests.append({'insertText': {'location': {'index': start}, 'text': new_text}})\n    requests.append({'deleteContentRange': {'range': {'startIndex': start, 'endIndex': end}}})\n\n# Requests must execute from bottom to top, with delete then insert at each range.\n# We constructed insert/delete pairs; regroup and sort by start descending.\npairs = []\nfor c in changes:\n    old = c['old']\n    repl = replacement_for(old)\n    leading_ws, prefix_len, new_text = repl\n    start = c['start'] + leading_ws\n    end = c['end'] - 1\n    pairs.append((start, end, new_text, old, (' ' * leading_ws) + new_text))\n\nbatched = []\nfor start, end, new_text, old, new in sorted(pairs, key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True):\n    batched.append({'deleteContentRange': {'range': {'startIndex': start, 'endIndex': end}}})\n    batched.append({'insertText': {'location': {'index': start}, 'text': new_text}})\n\nif batched:\n    docs.documents().batchUpdate(documentId=mod.DOC_ID, body={'requests': batched}).execute()\n\nprint(json.dumps({\n    'status': 'applied' if batched else 'noop',\n    'changed_count': len(pairs),\n    'changes_preview': [{'old': old, 'new': new} for _, _, _, old, new in pairs[:50]],\n}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))\n"}
```

Never write Slack/email-derived items into the Daily ToM doc without DJ's explicit approval.\n3. Suggested ToM additions are approval-required suggestions only.\n4. Most important first. Decisive, not neutral.\n2. **Needs DJ attention** — items requiring a decision, response, or action. Opinionated: tell DJ what to do.\n3. **Worth knowing / monitor** — brief. No action today. One paragraph or less per item.\n4. **Deliberately excluded** — one-liner list showing what the filter caught and why. Builds trust.\n5. **Suggested ToM additions — approval required** — formatted ToM-style entries. Ask DJ directly for each.\n6. **Watchlist / carry-forward** — compact rolling state for next briefing.\n\nSee `references/slack-filter-calibration.md` for detailed content filtering rules.\n\n## Calibration rules (v3 — validated [REDACTED_PHONE])\n\n### The DJ relevance test\nEvery item must answer: **\"Why does DJ need to know this, and what decision or action follows?\"**\nIf the answer is weak → downgrade to \"Worth knowing\" or exclude. Do not fill the briefing with items that merely exist.\n\n### Hard exclusions (never include)\n- **Slackbot app install requests** — DJ is a workspace super admin and receives these automatically. They are IT's job. Hard-exclude at filter level, never surface in briefing.\n- **Pure bot test results / CI channels** — waves-dev-test-automation, test-android-results, backend-dev-testify-automation, application-status, plattest-012n3.\n- **Cleaning/housekeeping ops channels** — below DJ level.\n- **Platform degradation bot floods** — repeated bot-only degraded/outage lines with no escalation owner or recovery status.\n\n### Always include\n- **DMs and MPIMs** — always surface. But MUST read full context (all thread/prior messages) before interpreting. Never quote one line and guess meaning.\n- **Exact Daily ToM entities** — Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, Olaya, Hubspot, F&B, Board deck, etc.\n- **Deal/acquisition/project channels** — proj-society-wynwood-acquisition, proj-mena, proj-easton, and similar.\n- **Money, legal, approval, deadline, investor, board signals** — wire, drawdown, capital, contract, signature, DD, due diligence, data room.\n\n### Include with judgment\n- **Tour/prospect signals in leasing channels** — people waiting for tours, prospect visits, staffing gaps at open houses. These ARE valuable to DJ even though \"leasing\" is otherwise a weak keyword.\n- **Engineering items with a named ToM entity** — product naming decisions (e.g., \"Society Wynwood → Flow Wynwood\"), blockers, or product decisions linked to active deals.\n- **Notable engineering discussions** — include when there is a real decision or entity; exclude low-context chatter (PR merges, minor code comments, identity creation, color fixes).\n\n### DM / group-DM handling\n- Always surface DMs/MPIMs.\n- Read **all context lines** provided before drawing any conclusion.\n- If a message is ambiguous without context (e.g., \"let's wind him down the week after next\"), report: what you know (participants, instruction given), what you don't know (subject), and provide the link. **Do not guess.**\n- If context is insufficient: \"Group DM with [names] mentions [instruction] — subject not identifiable from available context. Link: [url].\"\n\n### False inference rule\n- **Never connect unrelated items** as if they are related.\n- Example: A Vercel access approval for a MENA app does NOT mean MENA KPI/Hubspot/revenue diagnostic work is unblocked — they are completely separate. Only state connections that are **explicitly** supported by the source mat

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something wrong, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <either \"none unless this repeats\" or specific action required>\n``​`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- \"DJ action\" = either \"none, Hermes should fix this\" or an explicit ask. Never omit this line.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Operational health check pattern\n\nFor monitoring several file freshness checks + script probes in Python:\n\n``​`python\nISSUES: list[str] = []\nLINES: list[str] = []\n\ndef check_file(path, max_age_h=None, max_size_kb=None, min_size=1):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if not p.exists():\n        ISSUES.append(f\"Missing file: {path}\")\n        LINES.append(f\"{path}: MISSING\")\n        return\n    age = (time.time() - p.stat().st_mtime) / 3600\n    LINES.append(f\"{path}: {p.stat().st_size} bytes, age {age:.1f}h\")\n    if p.stat().st_size < min_size:\n        ISSUES.append(f\"File appears empty: {path}\")\n    if max_age_h and age > max_age_h:\n        I

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something wrong, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <either \"none unless this repeats\" or specific action required>\n``​`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- \"DJ action\" = either \"none, Hermes should fix this\" or an explicit ask. Never omit this line.\n\n---\n\n## Alert dedup / rate-limiting pattern\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n``​`yaml\nscript: path/to/script.sh   # relative to /opt/data/scripts/ or absolute\nno_agent: true               # script output IS the delivery; no LLM turn\ndeliver: telegram:<chat>:<topic>  # or \"local\" for disk only\nschedule: \"*/30 * * * *\"    # 5-field cron or \"every 30m\" / \"every 2h\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Operational health check pattern\n\nFor monitoring several file freshness checks + script probes in Python:\n\n``​`python\nISSUES: list[str] = []\nLINES: list[str] = []\n\ndef check_file(path, max_age_h=None, max_size_kb=None, min_size=1):\n    p = Path(path)\n    if not p.exists():\n        ISSUES.append(f\"Missing file: {path}\")\n        LINES.append(f\"{path}: MISSING\")\n        return\n    age = (time.time() - p.stat().st_mtime) / 3600\n    LINES.append(f\"{path}: {p.stat().st_size} bytes, age {age:.1f}h\")\n    if p.stat().st_size < min_size:\n        ISSUES.append(f\"File appears empty: {path}\")\n    if max_age_h and age > max_age_h:\n        I

```text
You’re right. That was contradictory.

What caused it:
- `chief_health_check.sh` treated any failed nested operational check as “Chief needs attention.”
- But the actual nested issue was the smart briefing being stale while its cron jobs are intentionally paused.
- Then it ended with “DJ action: none,” which makes the alert framing wrong.

I fixed it:

- If something actually needs you:  
  ``​`text
  ⚠️ Chief needs DJ attention
  ``​`
- If it’s internal / non-DJ-actionable:  
  ``​`text
  ℹ️ Chief Hermes follow-up logged
  DJ action: none.
  ``​`
- If smart briefing is stale **because the briefing cron jobs are paused**, that is now skipped as an issue:
  ``​`text
  Smart briefing freshness: skipped because smart briefing cron jobs are paused
  ``​`

Verified current output now says:

``​`text
✅ Chief OK
DJ action: none.
``​`

And the operational health check now ends with:

``​`text
Status: OK
``​`

I also updated the daily morning health summary prompt so it does **not** say “attention needed” unless there is actual DJ action required.
```
